<a id='question-070'></a>

### Question 070: Linkage Disequilibrium & Pharmacogenetics
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Linkage disequilibrium (LD) measures the non-random association of alleles at different loci. It's quantified by the coefficient D = P(AB) - P(A)P(B). Understanding LD is crucial in pharmacogenetics, where variations in genes encoding drug metabolizing enzymes or drug targets can influence drug response. (连锁不平衡 (LD) 衡量不同基因座上等位基因的非随机关联。其量化系数为 D = P(AB) - P(A)P(B)。理解 LD 在药代动力学中至关重要，因为编码药物代谢酶或药物靶标基因的变异会影响药物反应。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old African American male with a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia is started on clopidogrel (Plavix) following a recent myocardial infarction. Clopidogrel is a prodrug that requires activation by the cytochrome P450 enzyme CYP2C19 to exert its antiplatelet effect. The patient experiences recurrent cardiovascular events despite adherence to the prescribed dose. Genetic testing reveals that the patient is homozygous for the CYP2C19*2 allele, which encodes an enzyme with significantly reduced activity compared to the wild-type CYP2C19*1 allele. A population genetics study in a large cohort of African Americans reveals the following allele frequencies for two nearby single nucleotide polymorphisms (SNPs) in the CYP2C19 gene:
- SNP1 (A/G): Frequency of A allele (P(A)) = 0.7
- SNP2 (C/T): Frequency of C allele (P(C)) = 0.6
In this cohort, the frequency of the haplotype AC (A allele at SNP1 and C allele at SNP2) is observed to be 0.35. Calculate the coefficient of linkage disequilibrium (D) between SNP1 and SNP2 in this population.

(A) -0.07
(B) 0.07
(C) 0.17
(D) 0.27
(E) 0.42

#### Correct Answer: (B)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The patient's poor response to clopidogrel is linked to the CYP2C19*2 allele, highlighting the importance of pharmacogenetics.
- The question focuses on linkage disequilibrium (LD) between two SNPs in the CYP2C19 gene.
- The frequencies of individual alleles (P(A) and P(C)) are provided.
- The frequency of the AC haplotype (P(AB) in the LD formula) is given as 0.35.
- The question asks to calculate the coefficient of linkage disequilibrium (D).

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- Linkage disequilibrium (LD) describes the non-random association of alleles at different loci. Two loci are in equilibrium when the frequency of any haplotype combination equals the product of the frequencies of the individual alleles at each locus.
- The coefficient of linkage disequilibrium (D) quantifies this association. The formula is D = P(AB) - P(A)P(B), where P(AB) is the frequency of the haplotype where allele A is present at locus 1 and allele B is present at locus 2, and P(A) and P(B) are the frequencies of alleles A and B, respectively.
- In this case:
    - Locus 1: SNP1 (A/G)
    - Locus 2: SNP2 (C/T)
    - Allele A at SNP1: P(A) = 0.7
    - Allele C at SNP2: P(C) = 0.6
    - Haplotype AC: P(AB) = 0.35
- Calculate D: D = P(AC) - P(A)P(C) = 0.35 - (0.7 * 0.6) = 0.35 - 0.42 = -0.07.
- A negative D value indicates that the haplotype AC is less frequent than expected if the loci were in equilibrium. The haplotype AG would be more frequent than expected.
- LD is important in pharmacogenetics because SNPs in linkage disequilibrium with a known functional variant (like the CYP2C19*2 allele) can serve as genetic markers for that variant, even if the marker SNP itself is not directly causal. The patient's poor response is due to the CYP2C19*2 allele, which is likely in LD with one or both of the SNPs studied.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) -0.07: This is the correct calculation: D = 0.35 - (0.7 * 0.6) = 0.35 - 0.42 = -0.07. Students might make a calculation error or misinterpret the formula.
- (C) 0.17: This might result from incorrectly calculating P(A)P(C) as 0.35 - 0.17 = 0.18, or from misinterpreting the formula. It doesn't represent the correct calculation.
- (D) 0.27: This value doesn't correspond to any logical calculation based on the provided data. It might be a random guess or a result of confusing the formula or the given values.
- (E) 0.42: This is the value of P(A)P(C) (0.7 * 0.6 = 0.42), not the coefficient D. Students might forget to subtract P(A)P(C) from P(AB).

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- Linkage disequilibrium (LD) measures the non-random association of alleles at different loci. The coefficient D = P(AB) - P(A)P(B) quantifies this association.
- LD is a fundamental concept in population genetics and pharmacogenetics, explaining why certain genetic markers can predict disease risk or drug response.
- Differential diagnosis: This question tests knowledge of LD calculation, not specific enzyme kinetics (like Michaelis-Menten), metabolic pathways (like glycolysis or TCA cycle), or specific genetic syndromes (like PKU or sickle cell anemia). It's purely a population genetics/pharmacogenetics calculation.

<a id='question-071'></a>

### Question 071: Huntington Disease
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Trinucleotide repeat expansion disorders, specifically Huntington disease (HD), caused by CAG repeat expansion in the *HTT* gene, leading to polyglutamine tract expansion in the huntingtin protein, resulting in neurodegeneration. (三核苷酸重复序列疾病，特别是亨廷顿病，由 *HTT* 基因中的 CAG 重复序列扩增引起，导致亨廷顿蛋白中聚谷氨酰胺片段扩增，最终导致神经退行性变。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of progressive involuntary movements (chorea) and cognitive decline over the past 3 years. His father was diagnosed with Huntington disease at age 50 and died at age 65 due to complications of the disease. The patient's mother was unaffected. On neurological examination, the patient exhibits choreiform movements, dysarthria, and impaired executive function. A family pedigree review reveals that the patient's paternal grandfather also had Huntington disease. Genetic testing is performed on the patient. The results show an expanded CAG repeat length in the *HTT* gene. The patient's sister, who is currently asymptomatic at age 38, undergoes genetic testing and is found to have a CAG repeat length of 28. The patient's CAG repeat length is 45.

Which of the following molecular mechanisms best explains the inheritance pattern and pathogenesis of Huntington disease in this family?

(A) Anticipation due to methylation changes in the *HTT* gene promoter region, leading to decreased expression of the mutant allele in subsequent generations.
(B) Anticipation due to expansion of the CAG repeat in the *HTT* gene during oogenesis or spermatogenesis, leading to earlier onset and increased severity in subsequent generations.
(C) Genomic imprinting of the *HTT* gene, where only the paternally inherited allele is expressed, and the CAG repeat expansion occurs preferentially on the paternal chromosome.
(D) Loss of heterozygosity (LOH) of the normal *HTT* allele, leading to overexpression of the mutant allele and a dominant negative effect on huntingtin protein function.
(E) A frameshift mutation in the *HTT* gene caused by a single nucleotide polymorphism (SNP) near the CAG repeat, resulting in a premature stop codon and a truncated huntingtin protein.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Presentation:** 45-year-old male with chorea, cognitive decline, onset at age 42 (3 years prior). This is consistent with Huntington disease (HD).
*   **Family History:** Father diagnosed at 50, died at 65. Paternal grandfather also affected. Mother unaffected. This strongly suggests an autosomal dominant inheritance pattern. The father's onset (50) is later than the patient's onset (42), suggesting potential anticipation.
*   **Pedigree:** Autosomal dominant pattern (affected father, affected grandfather, patient affected, unaffected mother, asymptomatic sister).
*   **Genetic Testing:** Expanded CAG repeat in the *HTT* gene in the patient (45 repeats). Sister has 28 repeats (normal range is typically <26 repeats; 27-35 is intermediate/reduced penetrance; >36 is full penetrance).
*   **Key Clue:** The difference in age of onset between the father (50) and the patient (42) points towards *anticipation*, a phenomenon where the disease presents earlier and/or more severely in successive generations.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
*   Huntington disease is caused by an expansion of a CAG trinucleotide repeat within the first exon of the *HTT* gene on chromosome 4.
*   The normal *HTT* allele typically has 10-35 CAG repeats.
*   Alleles with 36-39 repeats show reduced penetrance, meaning not everyone with this number of repeats will develop the disease, and onset may be later.
*   Alleles with ≥40 CAG repeats are associated with full penetrance and disease onset, typically between ages 30-50.
*   The patient has 45 CAG repeats, consistent with full penetrance and onset in his 40s. His sister has 28 repeats, which is within the normal range.
*   The phenomenon of *anticipation* in HD is primarily due to the instability of the CAG repeat during transmission from one generation to the next, particularly during spermatogenesis. The repeat number tends to increase, leading to earlier onset and/or increased severity in subsequent generations. This expansion is thought to occur due to slippage of the DNA polymerase during replication.
*   The expanded CAG repeat leads to an expanded polyglutamine (polyQ) tract in the N-terminal region of the huntingtin protein. This elongated polyQ tract causes the protein to misfold, aggregate, and form intranuclear inclusions, leading to neuronal dysfunction and death, particularly in the striatum and cortex.
*   HD is inherited in an autosomal dominant manner. The disease is caused by a gain-of-function mutation (toxic gain) due to the expanded polyQ tract, not a loss-of-function.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Anticipation due to methylation changes in the *HTT* gene promoter region, leading to decreased expression of the mutant allele in subsequent generations.** Methylation changes can influence gene expression, but anticipation in HD is primarily driven by repeat expansion, not methylation changes leading to *decreased* expression of the mutant allele. In fact, the mutant allele is often *overexpressed* due to the expanded polyQ tract. Methylation changes are more characteristic of imprinting disorders.
*   **(C) Genomic imprinting of the *HTT* gene, where only the paternally inherited allele is expressed, and the CAG repeat expansion occurs preferentially on the paternal chromosome.** HD is *not* an imprinted disorder. Both alleles are expressed. While there is evidence that paternal transmission is associated with a greater tendency for repeat expansion (leading to anticipation), the gene itself is not imprinted.
*   **(D) Loss of heterozygosity (LOH) of the normal *HTT* allele, leading to overexpression of the mutant allele and a dominant negative effect on huntingtin protein function.** LOH is not the primary mechanism of HD pathogenesis. The disease is caused by the presence of the expanded repeat on *one* allele, leading to the production of a toxic mutant protein. While somatic mosaicism can occur, LOH of the normal allele is not the fundamental cause of the disease or anticipation.
*   **(E) A frameshift mutation in the *HTT* gene caused by a single nucleotide polymorphism (SNP) near the CAG repeat, resulting in a premature stop codon and a truncated huntingtin protein.** HD is caused by a *trinucleotide repeat expansion*, not a frameshift mutation or a premature stop codon. The mutation is a gain-of-function due to the expanded polyQ tract, not a loss-of-function due to a truncated protein.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
*   **High-Yield Takeaway:** Trinucleotide repeat expansion disorders like Huntington disease exhibit anticipation, where the disease severity increases, and the age of onset decreases in successive generations, primarily due to instability of the repeat during gametogenesis.
*   **Differentials:** Other trinucleotide repeat disorders include Fragile X syndrome (CGG repeat in *FMR1*), Myotonic dystrophy (CTG repeat in *DMPK*), and Spinocerebellar ataxias (various repeats). Understand the specific gene, repeat sequence, and clinical features of each. Also, differentiate from other causes of chorea (e.g., Sydenham's chorea, drug-induced chorea, Wilson's disease).

<a id='question-072'></a>

### Question 072: Pharmacogenetics - Cytochrome P450 Metabolism & SNPs
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Single nucleotide polymorphisms (SNPs) in genes encoding drug-metabolizing enzymes, specifically cytochrome P450s (CYPs), can alter enzyme activity, leading to variable drug response or toxicity. (单核苷酸多态性 (SNPs) 会影响编码药物代谢酶的基因，特别是细胞色素 P450 (CYPs)，从而改变酶活性，导致药物反应或毒性变化。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male with a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia is prescribed simvastatin (a statin drug metabolized by CYP3A4) for his elevated LDL cholesterol. He is also taking clopidogrel (an antiplatelet drug activated by CYP2C19) for secondary prevention after a recent myocardial infarction. One week after starting simvastatin at a standard dose of 40 mg daily, the patient presents to the emergency department with severe myalgia, muscle weakness, and dark urine (consistent with rhabdomyolysis). His creatine kinase (CK) level is markedly elevated at 15,000 U/L (normal < 200 U/L). His serum creatinine is also elevated at 2.5 mg/dL (normal 0.7-1.3 mg/dL), suggesting acute kidney injury secondary to myoglobinuria. Genetic testing reveals that the patient is homozygous for a specific SNP in the *CYP3A4* gene (rs4773646, resulting in the CYP3A4*1A variant) and heterozygous for a loss-of-function SNP in the *CYP2C19* gene (rs4986893, resulting in the CYP2C19*2 allele). Which of the following molecular mechanisms best explains the patient's severe adverse reaction to simvastatin?

(A) The *CYP2C19* loss-of-function allele leads to decreased activation of clopidogrel, resulting in inadequate antiplatelet effect and subsequent thrombosis, which indirectly causes muscle damage.
(B) The *CYP3A4* SNP results in increased CYP3A4 enzyme activity, leading to accelerated metabolism of simvastatin and decreased plasma levels, paradoxically causing muscle toxicity due to reduced statin exposure.
(C) The *CYP3A4* SNP results in decreased CYP3A4 enzyme activity, leading to reduced metabolism of simvastatin, resulting in increased plasma levels and accumulation of the drug, which causes muscle toxicity.
(D) The *CYP2C19* loss-of-function allele leads to decreased metabolism of simvastatin, resulting in increased plasma levels and accumulation of the drug, which causes muscle toxicity.
(E) The patient's severe myalgia and rhabdomyolysis are unrelated to the SNPs and are likely due to an idiosyncratic drug reaction to simvastatin.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 55-year-old male with hypertension, hyperlipidemia, recent MI, taking simvastatin and clopidogrel.
- **Adverse Event:** Severe myalgia, muscle weakness, dark urine, markedly elevated CK (15,000 U/L), elevated creatinine (2.5 mg/dL) one week after starting simvastatin 40 mg. This clinical picture is classic for statin-induced rhabdomyolysis.
- **Genetic Findings:** Homozygous for *CYP3A4* SNP (rs4773646, CYP3A4*1A variant) and heterozygous for *CYP2C19* loss-of-function SNP (rs4986893, CYP2C19*2 allele).
- **Drug Metabolism:** Simvastatin is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4. Clopidogrel is a prodrug activated by CYP2C19.
- **Question:** Mechanism explaining the severe reaction to simvastatin.

The key clue is the severe rhabdomyolysis associated with simvastatin use and the specific *CYP3A4* SNP identified. The question asks for the mechanism explaining the *simvastatin* toxicity.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **Simvastatin Metabolism:** Simvastatin is a prodrug that requires hydroxylation by CYP3A4 to form its active metabolite, simvastatin acid. This active metabolite is a potent inhibitor of HMG-CoA reductase.
- **CYP3A4 Polymorphisms:** The *CYP3A4* gene exhibits significant genetic polymorphism. The rs4773646 SNP (leading to the CYP3A4*1A variant) is associated with *decreased* CYP3A4 enzyme activity compared to the wild-type CYP3A4*1B allele. Being homozygous for this variant (*1A/*1A) further reduces CYP3A4 activity.
- **Mechanism of Toxicity:** Reduced CYP3A4 activity due to the *1A/*1A genotype leads to decreased metabolism of simvastatin. This results in higher plasma concentrations of simvastatin, increased formation of the active metabolite (simvastatin acid), and consequently, increased inhibition of HMG-CoA reductase in muscle tissue. This excessive inhibition leads to muscle toxicity, manifesting as myalgia, weakness, and rhabdomyolysis (indicated by high CK and myoglobinuria causing kidney injury).
- **Clopidogrel Metabolism:** Clopidogrel is also a prodrug activated by CYP2C19. The *CYP2C19* loss-of-function allele (*2) leads to reduced activation of clopidogrel, resulting in decreased antiplatelet effect. While this is clinically relevant for cardiovascular outcomes, it does not explain the simvastatin-induced rhabdomyolysis.
- **Conclusion:** The patient's severe simvastatin toxicity is directly attributable to the reduced metabolism of simvastatin caused by the *CYP3A4* SNP (*1A/*1A), leading to drug accumulation and muscle damage.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): This option incorrectly links the *CYP2C19* SNP to the simvastatin toxicity. The *CYP2C19* SNP affects clopidogrel metabolism, potentially leading to reduced antiplatelet effect, not simvastatin toxicity. Thrombosis is not the mechanism of rhabdomyolysis here.
- (B): This option incorrectly states that the *CYP3A4* SNP leads to *increased* enzyme activity. The rs4773646 SNP (*1A) is associated with *decreased* CYP3A4 activity. Furthermore, increased metabolism would lead to lower drug levels, which is contrary to the mechanism of statin toxicity (accumulation).
- (D): This option incorrectly links the *CYP2C19* SNP to simvastatin metabolism. Simvastatin is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4, not CYP2C19. The *CYP2C19* SNP affects clopidogrel activation.
- (E): While idiosyncratic drug reactions can occur, the presence of a known SNP associated with altered simvastatin metabolism provides a clear, plausible, and genetically confirmed mechanism for the observed toxicity. Attributing it solely to an idiosyncratic reaction ignores the significant genetic information provided. Pharmacogenetic interactions are common causes of adverse drug reactions.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** SNPs in drug-metabolizing enzymes (like CYPs) can significantly alter drug pharmacokinetics (absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion) and pharmacodynamics (drug effect), leading to variable drug response or toxicity. Simvastatin is metabolized by CYP3A4, and reduced CYP3A4 activity can lead to drug accumulation and myotoxicity.
- **Differentials:** Other causes of rhabdomyolysis include drug interactions (e.g., simvastatin + certain antifungals or macrolides inhibiting CYP3A4), hypothyroidism, trauma, strenuous exercise, and genetic muscle disorders. However, the vignette specifically points to a genetic polymorphism affecting simvastatin metabolism as the cause. Other CYP3A4 inhibitors could also cause this, but the question focuses on the SNP's effect.

<a id='question-073'></a>

### Question 073: Linkage Analysis - Informative Mating (Scenario 2)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Linkage analysis requires informative matings. An informative mating for two loci (disease gene and polymorphic marker) occurs when at least one parent is heterozygous for both loci (doubly heterozygous). This allows for the observation of recombination events between the two loci, enabling the calculation of a recombination frequency and LOD score. Scenario 2 specifically tests the requirement for *both* parents to be heterozygous at *both* loci for maximum informativeness, especially when dealing with recessive disorders. (连锁分析需要信息量大的交配。两个基因座（致病基因和多态性标记基因座）的信息量大的交配是指至少一个亲本对这两个基因座都是杂合的（双杂合）。这允许观察两个基因座之间的重组事件，从而计算重组频率和LOD评分。情景2专门测试了两个亲本都必须在两个基因座上杂合才能获得最大信息量的要求，尤其是在处理隐性遗传病时。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old woman and her 38-year-old husband are seeking genetic counseling. They have a 6-year-old son with cystic fibrosis (CF), an autosomal recessive disorder caused by mutations in the *CFTR* gene. The couple is asymptomatic for CF. They are planning to have another child and are concerned about the recurrence risk. To assess the potential for prenatal diagnosis, the genetic counselor orders linkage analysis. The husband's DNA is tested for a highly polymorphic microsatellite marker (D7S8) located 5 cM from the *CFTR* gene on chromosome 7. The results show that the husband is heterozygous for D7S8, with alleles of 150 bp and 170 bp. The wife's DNA is also tested for D7S8, and she is found to be homozygous for the 150 bp allele. The son with CF is tested for D7S8 and is found to be heterozygous, with alleles of 150 bp and 170 bp. The wife is also found to be heterozygous for the *CFTR* mutation causing her son's CF. The husband's *CFTR* genotype is unknown but is assumed to be heterozygous carrier status, as he is asymptomatic.

Which of the following statements regarding the informativeness of this mating for linkage analysis between the *CFTR* gene and the D7S8 marker is correct?

(A) This mating is non-informative because the mother is homozygous for the D7S8 marker, preventing the observation of recombination events.
(B) This mating is informative because the son inherited alleles from both parents for both the *CFTR* gene and the D7S8 marker, allowing for the calculation of a recombination frequency.
(C) This mating is maximally informative because both parents are heterozygous for both the *CFTR* gene and the D7S8 marker, allowing for the observation of all four possible allele combinations in the offspring.
(D) This mating is informative only if the husband is heterozygous for the D7S8 marker, as the mother's homozygosity prevents observation of recombination events involving her alleles.
(E) This mating is non-informative because the son inherited the same D7S8 allele (150 bp) from his mother and the other allele (170 bp) from his father, indicating a linkage disequilibrium specific to this family.

#### Correct Answer: (B)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** A couple with one affected child (CF, autosomal recessive) seeks genetic counseling.
- **Goal:** Assess informativeness of the mating for linkage analysis between the *CFTR* gene and a polymorphic marker D7S8.
- **Key Genotypes:**
    - Husband: D7S8 heterozygous (150 bp / 170 bp), *CFTR* carrier (heterozygous, assumed).
    - Wife: D7S8 homozygous (150 bp / 150 bp), *CFTR* carrier (heterozygous).
    - Son (affected with CF): D7S8 heterozygous (150 bp / 170 bp), *CFTR* affected (homozygous recessive).
- **Question:** Determine the informativeness of this specific mating for linkage analysis.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **Linkage Analysis:** A statistical method used to determine the proximity of two genes (or a gene and a marker) on a chromosome. It relies on observing the co-inheritance of alleles.
- **Informativeness of Mating:** Depends on the genotypes of the parents at both loci.
    - **Maximally Informative:** Both parents are heterozygous for both loci (AaBbCcDd...). This allows observation of all four possible gamete combinations (AB, Ab, aB, ab) and all possible recombinant offspring phenotypes.
    - **Informative:** At least one parent is heterozygous for both loci, OR one parent is heterozygous for one locus and homozygous for the other, AND the other parent is heterozygous for the locus where the first parent is homozygous. This allows observation of some recombination events.
    - **Non-Informative:** Both parents are homozygous for both loci (AABBCCDD...) OR one parent is homozygous for both loci, and the other parent is homozygous for the other locus (AABB x aabb). No recombination events can be observed.
- **Applying to the Vignette:**
    - The wife is homozygous for D7S8 (150/150).
    - The husband is heterozygous for D7S8 (150/170).
    - The son is heterozygous for D7S8 (150/170).
    - The wife is heterozygous for *CFTR*.
    - The husband is heterozygous for *CFTR*.
    - The son is homozygous recessive for *CFTR*.
- **Analysis:** The wife is homozygous for D7S8 (150/150). This means she can only pass on the 150 bp allele. The son inherited the 150 bp allele from his mother and the 170 bp allele from his father. The husband is heterozygous for D7S8 (150/170). Since the mother is homozygous for D7S8, she cannot be heterozygous for *both* the *CFTR* gene and the D7S8 marker. The husband *is* heterozygous for both.
- **Informativeness Assessment:** Because the mother is homozygous for D7S8, she cannot contribute to the observation of recombination events *between* the D7S8 marker and the *CFTR* gene in her offspring. However, the husband is heterozygous for both, and the son inherited alleles from both parents. The son's genotype (150/170 for D7S8) confirms he received one allele from each parent. The fact that the son inherited the 150 bp allele from the mother (who is homozygous 150/150) and the 170 bp allele from the father (who is heterozygous 150/170) provides information about the linkage. If the D7S8 marker and *CFTR* gene were tightly linked, the 150 bp allele and the *CFTR* allele on the same chromosome would tend to be inherited together. Since the son has CF (inheriting the *CFTR* mutation from the mother) and the 170 bp D7S8 allele from the father, we know the son inherited the maternal chromosome 7 carrying the *CFTR* mutation and the paternal chromosome 7 carrying the 170 bp D7S8 allele. This allows for the calculation of recombination frequency between the *CFTR* gene and the D7S8 marker, making the mating informative. The key is that the son inherited alleles from *both* parents for *both* loci.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** While the mother's homozygosity for D7S8 limits the *types* of recombination events that can be observed *from her perspective*, the mating is still informative because the son inherited alleles from both parents for both loci. The father's heterozygosity for both loci, combined with the son's inheritance pattern, allows for linkage analysis. The mother's homozygosity makes the mating *not maximally* informative, but it does not make it non-informative.
- (B): **Correct.** The son inherited one allele for both the *CFTR* gene and the D7S8 marker from each parent. The father is heterozygous for both. The mother is homozygous for D7S8 but heterozygous for *CFTR*. The son's genotype (150/170 for D7S8, affected for *CFTR*) provides information about the linkage between the marker and the gene. Specifically, the son inherited the maternal *CFTR* allele (causing disease) and the paternal D7S8 allele (170 bp). The mother contributed the 150 bp D7S8 allele. This allows calculation of recombination frequency.
- (C): **Incorrect.** This mating is *not* maximally informative. Maximal informativeness requires *both* parents to be heterozygous for *both* loci. In this case, the mother is homozygous for the D7S8 marker (150/150).
- (D): **Incorrect.** The mother *is* homozygous for D7S8 (150/150), which does limit the observation of recombination events involving her alleles. However, the mating is still informative because the son inherited alleles from both parents, and the father is heterozygous for both loci. The statement implies the mother's homozygosity makes the mating non-informative, which is incorrect.
- (E): **Incorrect.** Linkage disequilibrium refers to the non-random association of alleles at different loci. While linkage disequilibrium can exist, the informativeness of the mating depends on the parents' genotypes. The fact that the son inherited specific alleles from each parent (150 from mother, 170 from father for D7S8) allows for linkage analysis, regardless of whether there is specific linkage disequilibrium in this family. The mating *is* informative.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Understand the criteria for informative matings in linkage analysis: at least one parent must be heterozygous for both loci. Maximal informativeness requires both parents to be heterozygous for both loci. Homozygosity in one parent for one locus limits, but does not necessarily eliminate, informativeness if the other parent is heterozygous for both loci and the offspring inherits alleles from both parents.
- **Differentials:** Distinguish between maximally informative, informative, and non-informative matings. Understand how parental genotypes (homozygous vs. heterozygous) affect the ability to observe recombination events and calculate linkage. Recognize that linkage analysis requires observing allele transmission from both parents to offspring.

---

<a id='question-074'></a>

### Question 074: Uninformative Matings: Parent Being Homozygous at the Marker Locus Preventing Tracking of Which Chromosomal Homolog Was Inherited - Clinical Scenario 2
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Uninformative matings occur when one parent is homozygous for a genetic marker, preventing the determination of which parental chromosome homolog was inherited by the offspring. This concept is crucial for understanding linkage analysis and pedigree interpretation. (不信息配对：当一个亲本在某个遗传标记位点为纯合子时，无法确定后代继承的是哪个亲本的同源染色体，这对于理解连锁分析和家谱解读至关重要。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old woman, Sarah, presents to a genetic clinic concerned about the risk of her child inheriting cystic fibrosis (CF). Sarah's husband, David, has a family history of CF, with his brother affected. Sarah herself has no family history of CF, but carrier screening revealed she is heterozygous for the common ΔF508 mutation in the CFTR gene. David underwent carrier screening, and the results showed he is homozygous for a rare CFTR mutation, R117H, which is known to cause mild symptoms in some individuals but is generally considered disease-causing. A pedigree analysis of David's family shows that his affected brother inherited the R117H mutation from their mother, who was heterozygous for R117H. David's father was unaffected and is known to be homozygous for a normal CFTR allele. Sarah and David are planning to conceive. They are considering preimplantation genetic diagnosis (PGD) to select embryos unaffected by CF. To facilitate PGD, they need to determine the specific CFTR alleles present on each of David's chromosomes. A nearby polymorphic marker, D7S1000, is linked to the CFTR gene on chromosome 7. Sarah is heterozygous for D7S1000 (alleles 150 bp and 175 bp). David is homozygous for D7S1000 (allele 150 bp). They have a child, Emily, who is unaffected by CF but is a carrier for the ΔF508 mutation (inherited from Sarah) and the R117H mutation (inherited from David). Emily's genotype for D7S1000 is heterozygous (alleles 150 bp and 175 bp). Based on this information, which of the following statements regarding the inheritance of the CFTR alleles and the D7S1000 marker in Emily is correct?

(A) Emily inherited the chromosome carrying the ΔF508 allele from her mother and the chromosome carrying the R117H allele from her father, and the D7S1000 allele 175 bp from her mother and the D7S1000 allele 150 bp from her father.
(B) Emily inherited the chromosome carrying the ΔF508 allele from her mother and the chromosome carrying the R117H allele from her father, and the D7S1000 allele 150 bp from her mother and the D7S1000 allele 175 bp from her father.
(C) Emily inherited the chromosome carrying the ΔF508 allele from her mother and the chromosome carrying the R117H allele from her father, but it is impossible to determine which D7S1000 allele (150 bp or 175 bp) she inherited from each parent.
(D) Emily inherited the chromosome carrying the ΔF508 allele from her father and the chromosome carrying the R117H allele from her mother, and the D7S1000 allele 175 bp from her mother and the D7S1000 allele 150 bp from her father.
(E) Emily inherited the chromosome carrying the ΔF508 allele from her father and the chromosome carrying the R117H allele from her mother, and the D7S1000 allele 150 bp from her mother and the D7S1000 allele 175 bp from her father.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Information:** Sarah (32yo) is ΔF508/normal (carrier). David is R117H/R117H (homozygous for a rare CFTR mutation).
*   **Family History:** David's brother has CF (R117H/R117H). David's mother is R117H/normal (carrier). David's father is normal/normal.
*   **Marker Information:** D7S1000 is linked to CFTR on chromosome 7. Sarah is heterozygous D7S1000 (150 bp / 175 bp). David is homozygous D7S1000 (150 bp / 150 bp).
*   **Child Information:** Emily is unaffected but a carrier: ΔF508/R117H. Emily is heterozygous D7S1000 (150 bp / 175 bp).
*   **Question:** Determine the inheritance pattern of CFTR alleles and the D7S1000 marker in Emily.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
*   **CFTR Gene and Mutations:** Cystic fibrosis is caused by mutations in the CFTR gene, which encodes a chloride channel. ΔF508 is a common deletion, and R117H is a less common missense mutation.
*   **Inheritance of CFTR Alleles:** Emily inherited the ΔF508 allele from her mother (Sarah) and the R117H allele from her father (David). This is straightforward Mendelian inheritance.
*   **D7S1000 Marker:** D7S1000 is a polymorphic marker linked to CFTR. Markers are used in linkage analysis to track the inheritance of genes.
*   **Uninformative Matings:** The key concept here is an uninformative mating. David is homozygous for the D7S1000 marker (150 bp / 150 bp). This means he can only pass on the 150 bp allele to his offspring.
*   **Emily's D7S1000 Genotype:** Emily has the genotype 150 bp / 175 bp for D7S1000. She inherited one allele (150 bp) from her father (David) and one allele (175 bp) from her mother (Sarah).
*   **Determining Parental Origin:** Since David is homozygous for D7S1000 (150 bp), Emily *must* have inherited the 150 bp allele from him. Sarah is heterozygous (150 bp / 175 bp), so Emily inherited the 175 bp allele from her mother.
*   **Conclusion:** Emily inherited the ΔF508 allele from her mother and the R117H allele from her father. She inherited the D7S1000 allele 150 bp from her father and the D7S1000 allele 175 bp from her mother.
*   **Why this is an Uninformative Mating for David:** Because David is homozygous for D7S1000, we know he *always* passes on the 150 bp allele. We cannot use the D7S1000 marker to determine which of David's chromosomes (carrying R117H) was inherited by Emily. If David were heterozygous (e.g., 150 bp / 175 bp), then we could track whether Emily inherited the 150 bp allele (linked to R117H) or the 175 bp allele (linked to the normal allele) from him. Since he is homozygous, this tracking is impossible. The question asks about tracking *which chromosomal homolog was inherited*. Since David is homozygous for the marker, he only has one type of homolog to pass on regarding that marker. Therefore, it's impossible to determine which of his two R117H chromosomes (which are identical regarding the D7S1000 marker) was passed to Emily.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. This option correctly identifies the origin of the CFTR alleles but incorrectly assigns the D7S1000 alleles' parental origin. It assumes Emily inherited the 175 bp allele from her father, which is impossible since David is homozygous for 150 bp.
- (B): Incorrect. This option correctly identifies the origin of the CFTR alleles but incorrectly assigns the D7S1000 alleles' parental origin. It assumes Emily inherited the 150 bp allele from her father and the 175 bp allele from her mother. While the CFTR origin is correct, the D7S1000 origin is swapped.
- (D): Incorrect. This option incorrectly identifies the origin of the CFTR alleles. It states Emily inherited ΔF508 from her father and R117H from her mother, which is the opposite of the actual inheritance. It also incorrectly assigns the D7S1000 alleles' parental origin based on the incorrect CFTR allele assignment.
- (E): Incorrect. This option incorrectly identifies the origin of the CFTR alleles. It states Emily inherited ΔF508 from her father and R117H from her mother, which is the opposite of the actual inheritance. It also incorrectly assigns the D7S1000 alleles' parental origin based on the incorrect CFTR allele assignment.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Uninformative matings occur when one parent is homozygous for a marker locus, preventing the determination of which parental chromosome homolog was inherited by the offspring. This limits the power of linkage analysis. Key differential: Informative matings occur when both parents are heterozygous for the marker, allowing tracking of parental chromosome inheritance. This concept is tested in pedigree analysis and linkage questions.

<a id='question-075'></a>

### Question 075: Cystic Fibrosis and Linkage Disequilibrium
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Linkage disequilibrium (LD) describes the non-random association of alleles at different loci. In the context of genetic diseases, a specific disease-causing mutation can be found in linkage disequilibrium with a nearby polymorphic marker allele, meaning they are inherited together more often than expected by chance because they reside on the same chromosome (cis configuration). This phenomenon is crucial for genetic mapping and diagnosis. (连锁不平衡 (LD) 描述了不同基因座上等位基因的非随机关联。在遗传病中，特定的致病突变可能与附近的等位基因多态性标记呈连锁不平衡，这意味着它们由于位于同一条染色体上 (顺式构型) 而比偶然情况更经常一起遗传。这对遗传图谱和诊断至关重要。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old Caucasian woman presents to a genetics clinic for pre-conception counseling. She has a family history of cystic fibrosis (CF) in her brother, who was diagnosed at age 5 and died at age 28 due to respiratory failure. Her parents are both asymptomatic carriers of CF. The patient underwent genetic testing for CF carrier status. Her genotype at the CFTR locus is determined using a panel of sequence-specific oligonucleotide (SSO) probes designed to detect common CFTR mutations and associated polymorphic markers. The SSO analysis reveals that she carries the common ΔF508 mutation (c.1521_1523delCTT) on one CFTR allele. Importantly, the SSO analysis also detects a specific polymorphic marker allele, designated as Marker X, located approximately 5 cM (centiMorgans) away from the CFTR gene on chromosome 7. The patient's genotype for Marker X is homozygous for the allele designated 'X2'. Population studies in Caucasians show that the ΔF508 mutation is strongly associated with the X2 allele of Marker X (approximately 95% linkage disequilibrium). The patient's partner also undergoes carrier screening. His CFTR genotype is wild-type, and his genotype for Marker X is heterozygous, carrying both the X1 and X2 alleles.

Based on this information, what is the probability that the patient's offspring will inherit the CFTR allele carrying the ΔF508 mutation from the patient?

(A) 0%
(B) 25%
(C) 50%
(D) 75%
(E) 100%

#### Correct Answer: (C)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The patient is a carrier for the ΔF508 CFTR mutation. This means one of her CFTR alleles has this mutation, and the other is wild-type.
- The patient's partner is a carrier for CF (implied by the question asking about offspring inheriting the ΔF508 mutation from the patient, suggesting the partner *could* pass on a wild-type allele, but the question specifically asks about the patient's contribution). The partner's CFTR genotype is wild-type, meaning he has two normal CFTR alleles.
- The patient's genotype for Marker X is homozygous X2/X2.
- The ΔF508 mutation is in strong linkage disequilibrium (LD) with the X2 allele of Marker X (95%). This means that 95% of the time, the ΔF508 mutation and the X2 allele are inherited together on the same chromosome.
- The patient's partner's genotype for Marker X is heterozygous X1/X2.
- The question asks for the probability that the offspring inherits the CFTR allele carrying the ΔF508 mutation *from the patient*.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- The patient carries one normal CFTR allele and one allele with the ΔF508 mutation.
- The patient is homozygous for the X2 allele at Marker X.
- Due to the strong linkage disequilibrium (95%), it is highly probable (95%) that the patient's CFTR allele carrying the ΔF508 mutation is also carrying the X2 allele. Let's call this chromosome #1 (ΔF508/X2).
- The patient's other CFTR allele is wild-type. Since she is homozygous for X2, this wild-type allele must also be linked to the X2 allele. Let's call this chromosome #2 (Wild-type/X2).
- Therefore, the patient's genotype is (ΔF508/X2) and (Wild-type/X2).
- The patient can only pass on one of these two chromosomes to her offspring.
- The probability of passing on the (ΔF508/X2) chromosome is 1/2 (50%).
- The probability of passing on the (Wild-type/X2) chromosome is 1/2 (50%).
- The question asks specifically for the probability of the offspring inheriting the ΔF508 mutation *from the patient*. This corresponds to the probability of the patient passing on the (ΔF508/X2) chromosome.
- This probability is 1/2 or 50%.
- The partner's genotype is irrelevant for the probability of the offspring inheriting the ΔF508 mutation *from the patient*. The partner's genotype is wild-type/wild-type for CFTR and X1/X2 for Marker X. The offspring will inherit one chromosome from the patient and one from the partner. The question focuses solely on the contribution from the patient.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) 0%: Incorrect. The patient is a carrier for the ΔF508 mutation, so there is a non-zero probability she will pass it on.
- (B) 25%: Incorrect. This might be confused with the probability of the offspring being a carrier if the partner was also a carrier, or perhaps a misunderstanding of the marker information. The patient has a 50% chance of passing on the ΔF508 allele.
- (D) 75%: Incorrect. This value doesn't correspond to any standard Mendelian probability in this scenario. It might arise from misinterpreting the linkage disequilibrium percentage (95%) as a probability of inheritance, or incorrectly combining probabilities.
- (E) 100%: Incorrect. The patient has two alleles, and she randomly passes on one. The probability of passing on a specific allele is always 50% unless there are specific circumstances like non-disjunction (which is not indicated here).

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- High-Yield Takeaway: Understand the concept of linkage disequilibrium (LD) and how it relates the inheritance of a disease mutation with a nearby polymorphic marker. Recognize that a carrier parent has a 50% chance of passing on any specific allele they carry.
- Differentials: This question tests understanding of LD vs. complete linkage (where two loci are always inherited together, which is rare). It also requires distinguishing the probability of a parent passing on a specific allele (50%) from the probability of an offspring inheriting a specific genotype (which depends on both parents). This scenario is distinct from autosomal dominant inheritance (where affected individuals pass on the mutation 100% if heterozygous) or X-linked inheritance patterns.

---

<a id='question-076'></a>

### Question 076: Repulsion (Trans) Phase: Disease Mutation and Specific Marker Allele Residing on Opposite Homologous Chromosomes - Clinical Scenario 2
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding linkage analysis, recombination frequency, repulsion linkage (trans configuration), and how marker alleles on opposite homologous chromosomes relate to disease allele inheritance. (理解连锁分析、重组频率、排斥连锁（trans构型）以及同源染色体上不同位点标记等位基因与疾病等位基因的遗传关系。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old woman presents to a genetics clinic concerned about her risk of developing Huntington's disease (HD). Her father was diagnosed with HD at age 45 and died at age 58. Her mother does not have HD, and there is no family history of HD on her mother's side. The patient underwent genetic testing for the HD gene (HTT) located on chromosome 4. The test revealed she carries one expanded CAG repeat allele (HTT/CAG > 35) and one normal allele (HTT/CAG < 26). To determine the origin of the expanded allele, linkage analysis was performed using a polymorphic marker (D4S155) located 5 centimorgans (cM) away from the HTT gene on chromosome 4. The patient's genotype for D4S155 is heterozygous, with alleles 150 bp and 170 bp. Her father's genotype for D4S155 was homozygous 150 bp. Her mother's genotype for D4S155 was homozygous 170 bp. Based on these findings, which of the following statements regarding the inheritance of the expanded HTT allele and the D4S155 alleles in this patient is correct?

(A) The patient inherited the expanded HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele from her father, and the normal HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele from her mother.
(B) The patient inherited the expanded HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele from her father, and the normal HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele from her mother.
(C) The patient inherited the expanded HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele from her mother, and the normal HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele from her father.
(D) The patient inherited the expanded HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele from her mother, and the normal HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele from her father.
(E) The patient inherited the normal HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele from her father, and the expanded HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele from her mother.

#### Correct Answer: (D)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old woman, risk assessment for Huntington's disease (HD).
- **Family History:** Father diagnosed with HD (autosomal dominant inheritance). Mother unaffected.
- **Patient's Genotype (HTT):** Heterozygous for the HD gene (HTT/CAG > 35 and HTT/CAG < 26). This means she carries one disease allele (expanded CAG repeat) and one normal allele.
- **Marker Information:** D4S155 marker is 5 cM from the HTT gene on chromosome 4. The patient is heterozygous for D4S155 (150 bp and 170 bp alleles).
- **Parental Genotypes (D4S155):** Father is homozygous 150 bp. Mother is homozygous 170 bp.
- **Question:** Determine the origin of the expanded HTT allele and the D4S155 alleles in the patient.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **Huntington's Disease:** Autosomal dominant disorder caused by an expanded CAG trinucleotide repeat in the HTT gene.
- **Linkage Analysis:** Used to determine the origin of a disease allele by tracking linked markers. The closer a marker is to a gene, the lower the probability of recombination between them.
- **Recombination Frequency:** 5 cM distance means a 5% chance of recombination occurring between the HTT gene and the D4S155 marker during meiosis.
- **Patient's D4S155 Genotype:** The patient has alleles 150 bp and 170 bp. Since the father is homozygous 150 bp and the mother is homozygous 170 bp, the patient must have inherited one allele from each parent.
- **Determining Origin of Expanded HTT Allele:** The patient carries the expanded HTT allele. Her father had HD, so he must have passed on the expanded HTT allele. Her mother does not have HD, so she passed on the normal HTT allele.
- **Linking HTT and D4S155 Alleles:**
    - The father passed on the expanded HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele.
    - The mother passed on the normal HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele.
- **Repulsion Linkage (Trans Configuration):** The expanded HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele are on the same chromosome (inherited from the father). The normal HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele are on the other homologous chromosome (inherited from the mother). This is a repulsion or trans configuration because the two alleles of the marker (150 bp and 170 bp) are on chromosomes that carry different alleles of the gene of interest (expanded HTT and normal HTT).
- **Conclusion:** The patient inherited the expanded HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele from her mother, and the normal HTT allele and the 150 bp D4S155 allele from her father.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. This option states the patient inherited the 150 bp D4S155 allele from the father and the 170 bp allele from the mother. This is correct for the marker alleles, but it incorrectly assigns the expanded HTT allele to the father's contribution. The father passed on the expanded HTT allele, but also the 150 bp D4S155 allele. The mother passed on the normal HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele.
- (B): Incorrect. This option states the patient inherited the 170 bp D4S155 allele from the father. This is incorrect because the father is homozygous for the 150 bp allele.
- (C): Incorrect. This option states the patient inherited the expanded HTT allele from the mother. This is incorrect because the mother does not have HD and passed on the normal HTT allele.
- (E): Incorrect. This option states the patient inherited the 150 bp D4S155 allele from the father and the 170 bp allele from the mother. This is correct for the marker alleles, but it incorrectly assigns the normal HTT allele to the father's contribution. The father passed on the expanded HTT allele, but also the 150 bp D4S155 allele. The mother passed on the normal HTT allele and the 170 bp D4S155 allele.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Linkage analysis uses polymorphic markers close to a gene of interest to track the inheritance of disease alleles. Understanding the parental genotypes for both the gene and the marker allows determination of which chromosome carries the disease allele and which marker alleles are linked to it. Repulsion linkage occurs when the two alleles of a marker are on chromosomes carrying different alleles of the gene.
- **Differentials:** This question tests linkage analysis, which is crucial for mapping disease genes. It differentiates from simple Mendelian inheritance by incorporating marker information. It also contrasts with scenarios where recombination occurs, separating the disease allele from the linked marker allele. Understanding the concept of repulsion (trans) vs. coupling (cis) linkage is key.

---

<a id='question-077'></a>

### Question 077: Determining Phase in Human Pedigrees: Requirement for Multigenerational Information (Grandparents, Parents, Offspring) to Establish Linkage Phase - Clinical Scenario 2
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Determining linkage phase (cis vs. trans) in human pedigrees requires observing the segregation of two linked alleles through multiple generations, specifically including grandparents, parents, and affected offspring, to establish whether the alleles are inherited together on the same chromosome (cis) or on different chromosomes (trans). (确定人类家谱中连锁相 (顺式 vs. 反式) 需要观察两个连锁等位基因在多个世代中的分离情况，特别是包括祖父母、父母和受影响的后代，以确定等位基因是否在同一条染色体上一起遗传 (顺式) 还是在不同的染色体上遗传 (反式))

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old woman presents to a genetics clinic concerned about her family history of a rare autosomal recessive disorder, "Melanoma-prone Xeroderma Pigmentosum variant" (MP-XPv). Her father (her mother's father) had severe MP-XPv, characterized by extreme sun sensitivity, numerous skin cancers by age 30, and neurological degeneration. Her mother was unaffected but carried the MP-XPv mutation. Her husband is unaffected and has no family history of MP-XPv. They have a 5-year-old son who exhibits mild sun sensitivity and has developed a single basal cell carcinoma. The couple is considering having another child and wants to understand the risk.

Genetic testing reveals that the father (the patient's maternal grandfather) was homozygous for the MP-XPv mutation (genotype: *xp/xp*). The patient's mother was heterozygous (genotype: *Xp/xp*), where *Xp* represents the normal allele and *xp* represents the MP-XPv allele. The patient's husband is homozygous for the normal allele (*Xp/Xp*). The patient herself is heterozygous for the MP-XPv mutation (genotype: *Xp/xp*). The patient's son is affected with MP-XPv and is homozygous for the MP-XPv mutation (genotype: *xp/xp*).

To accurately counsel the couple about the recurrence risk in future pregnancies, it is crucial to determine the phase of the alleles on the mother's chromosome carrying the MP-XPv allele. The mother inherited one chromosome from her father (the patient's maternal grandfather) and one from her mother (the patient's maternal grandmother). The patient's maternal grandfather had genotype *xp/xp*. Therefore, the chromosome inherited by the patient's mother from her father must carry the *xp* allele. The patient's mother's genotype is *Xp/xp*. The patient's son inherited the *xp* allele from his mother and the *xp* allele from his father (who must have been a carrier, genotype *Xp/xp*, although this was not explicitly stated in the initial presentation, it is implied by the son's genotype *xp/xp* and the fact that the husband is *Xp/Xp*).

The patient's mother has genotype *Xp/xp*. The patient's son has genotype *xp/xp*. The patient's son inherited one *xp* allele from his mother. The question is: What is the phase of the alleles on the chromosome inherited by the patient's mother from her father (the patient's maternal grandfather)? Is the *Xp* allele on the same chromosome as the *xp* allele (cis configuration, *Xp xp*), or are they on different chromosomes (trans configuration, *Xp* from the mother and *xp* from the father)?

(A) The alleles are in *cis* configuration on the chromosome inherited by the mother from her father (*Xp xp*).
(B) The alleles are in *trans* configuration on the chromosome inherited by the mother from her father (*Xp* from the mother's mother and *xp* from the mother's father).
(C) The phase cannot be determined without knowing the genotype of the patient's maternal grandmother.
(D) The phase is irrelevant for calculating the recurrence risk in this case because the disorder is autosomal recessive.
(E) The phase is determined by the patient's son's genotype, which indicates the *Xp* allele must be on the chromosome inherited from the father.

#### Correct Answer: (A)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient's Maternal Grandfather:** Genotype *xp/xp* (homozygous affected). This means both chromosomes he carries have the *xp* allele.
*   **Patient's Mother:** Genotype *Xp/xp* (heterozygous carrier). She inherited one chromosome from her father (maternal grandfather) and one from her mother (maternal grandmother).
*   **Patient's Son:** Genotype *xp/xp* (affected). He inherited one *xp* allele from his mother and one *xp* allele from his father.
*   **Patient's Father (Husband):** Genotype *Xp/xp* (carrier). He must have contributed the *xp* allele to his son.
*   **Key Question:** What is the phase of the alleles on the chromosome the mother inherited from her father? This chromosome carries one *Xp* allele and one *xp* allele. The question is whether they are on the same chromosome (*Xp xp*, cis) or different chromosomes (*Xp* from the mother's mother and *xp* from the mother's father, trans).

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
*   **Linkage and Phase:** When two genes or alleles are located close together on the same chromosome, they are said to be linked. The phase refers to the arrangement of alleles on a single chromosome. If two alleles on the same chromosome are inherited together, they are in *cis* phase. If they are on different chromosomes (or inherited independently due to recombination), they are in *trans* phase.
*   **Determining Phase:** In human pedigrees, determining the phase of linked alleles requires observing their segregation through multiple generations. Specifically, we need to see how the alleles are transmitted from grandparents to parents to offspring.
*   **Applying to the Case:**
    *   The patient's maternal grandfather had genotype *xp/xp*.
    *   Therefore, the chromosome he passed to his daughter (the patient's mother) must carry the *xp* allele.
    *   The patient's mother's genotype is *Xp/xp*. This means she has one chromosome with the *Xp* allele and one chromosome with the *xp* allele.
    *   The chromosome she inherited from her father (the patient's maternal grandfather) must carry the *xp* allele.
    *   Since the patient's mother is *Xp/xp*, the chromosome she inherited from her father must be *Xp xp*. This is because the grandfather only had *xp* alleles to pass on.
    *   Therefore, the *Xp* allele and the *xp* allele are on the same chromosome (inherited from the grandfather), meaning they are in *cis* configuration.
*   **Why Multigenerational Information is Needed:** If we only knew the mother's genotype (*Xp/xp*) and the son's genotype (*xp/xp*), we couldn't determine the phase. The son inherited *xp* from his mother. We don't know if the *Xp* allele on the mother's chromosome came from her mother or her father. However, knowing the grandfather's genotype (*xp/xp*) allows us to definitively conclude that the chromosome inherited by the mother from her father carries the *xp* allele, and since the mother is *Xp/xp*, that chromosome must be *Xp xp*.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Correct.** As explained above, the maternal grandfather was *xp/xp*. He passed an *xp* allele to his daughter (the patient's mother). Since the mother is *Xp/xp*, the chromosome she inherited from her father must be *Xp xp*. Thus, the alleles are in *cis* configuration on that chromosome.
- (B): **Incorrect.** This option suggests a *trans* configuration on the chromosome inherited from the father. This is incorrect because the father (*xp/xp*) could only pass on a chromosome carrying the *xp* allele. The mother's genotype (*Xp/xp*) confirms she received an *xp* allele from her father. The *Xp* allele must be on the other chromosome, inherited from her mother. Therefore, the chromosome inherited from the father is *Xp xp* (cis).
- (C): **Incorrect.** The phase *can* be determined. Knowing the genotype of the maternal grandfather (*xp/xp*) provides the necessary information. The chromosome he passed to his daughter (the patient's mother) must carry the *xp* allele. Since the mother is *Xp/xp*, this chromosome must be *Xp xp*.
- (D): **Incorrect.** While the disorder is autosomal recessive, determining the phase is crucial for accurate genetic counseling, especially if considering prenatal diagnosis or preimplantation genetic diagnosis (PGD). Knowing the phase allows for more precise calculation of the probability of having an affected child, although in this specific case, the mother is *Xp/xp* and the father is *Xp/xp*, so the risk of an affected child (*xp/xp*) is 25%. However, the question asks about the phase determination itself, which is relevant regardless of the specific recurrence risk calculation. The phase is important because it tells us which alleles are linked on the same chromosome.
- (E): **Incorrect.** The patient's son inherited one *xp* allele from his mother. The phase on the *mother's* chromosome inherited from her *father* is what is being asked. We know this chromosome is *Xp xp* because the grandfather was *xp/xp*. The son's genotype doesn't directly determine the phase on the chromosome inherited by the mother from her father; rather, the grandfather's genotype combined with the mother's genotype allows us to deduce the phase.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Determining linkage phase in human pedigrees requires observing allele segregation across multiple generations, including grandparents, parents, and offspring. The genotype of homozygous individuals (like the maternal grandfather) is key to establishing the phase on chromosomes inherited by their heterozygous offspring. This concept is crucial for accurate genetic counseling and understanding the inheritance patterns of linked genes or alleles. Differentiating this from simple Mendelian inheritance patterns (where phase is not typically considered) is important.

<a id='question-078'></a>

### Question 078: Haplotype Concept: A Set of Closely Linked Genetic Markers or Alleles Inherited Together on a Single Chromosome as a Unit - Clinical Scenario 2
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the concept of haplotypes, linkage disequilibrium, and how specific haplotypes are associated with drug metabolism phenotypes, particularly variations in CYP2C19 and their impact on clopidogrel efficacy. (理解连锁不平衡、单倍型、以及特定单倍型与药物代谢表型（如CYP2C19变异与氯吡格雷疗效）之间的关系。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of coronary artery disease status post percutaneous coronary intervention (PCI) with stent placement 6 months ago presents to the emergency department complaining of severe chest pain radiating to his left arm, consistent with unstable angina. He is currently taking aspirin 81 mg daily, atorvastatin 40 mg daily, and metoprolol 50 mg twice daily. His EKG shows ST-segment depression in leads V4-V6. He is admitted to the cardiac care unit and started on dual antiplatelet therapy (DAPT) with aspirin and clopidogrel 75 mg daily.

Three days later, he develops symptoms consistent with another acute coronary syndrome (ACS), including recurrent chest pain and elevated troponin levels. His cardiologist orders a platelet function assay (PFA-100) which shows prolonged closure time (CT) of 350 seconds (normal range: 180-360 seconds), suggesting adequate platelet inhibition. However, his symptoms persist, and repeat EKG shows worsening ST-segment depression. A review of his medications reveals no recent changes or interactions. Given the recurrent ACS despite DAPT and seemingly adequate platelet inhibition by aspirin (confirmed by PFA-100), the cardiologist suspects clopidogrel resistance or non-adherence.

Genetic testing is performed, revealing the patient's genotype for the *CYP2C19* gene as *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17. The *CYP2C19*2 allele is associated with loss-of-function, while the *CYP2C19*17 allele is associated with gain-of-function. Which of the following best explains the patient's recurrent ACS despite clopidogrel therapy?

(A) The patient's *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype leads to increased production of the active metabolite of clopidogrel, resulting in excessive platelet inhibition and paradoxical thrombosis.
(B) The patient's *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype represents a compound heterozygote state where one allele confers poor metabolism and the other confers extensive metabolism, leading to intermediate clopidogrel activation and potentially reduced efficacy.
(C) The patient's *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype is a common haplotype associated with increased sensitivity to statins, leading to myopathy which is misinterpreted as ACS.
(D) The patient's *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype is a common haplotype associated with increased risk of stent thrombosis due to impaired endothelial function.
(E) The patient's *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype is a common haplotype associated with increased risk of bleeding complications due to enhanced platelet aggregation.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Presentation:** 62M with CAD, recent PCI, recurrent ACS despite DAPT (aspirin + clopidogrel).
*   **Lab Findings:** PFA-100 CT is within normal limits (350s), suggesting adequate aspirin effect. Recurrent ACS (chest pain, elevated troponin, EKG changes) points to inadequate clopidogrel effect.
*   **Genetic Testing:** *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype. *CYP2C19*2 is a loss-of-function allele (poor metabolizer), and *CYP2C19*17 is a gain-of-function allele (ultrarapid metabolizer).
*   **Question:** Explain the recurrent ACS despite clopidogrel.

The key clue is the patient's genotype (*CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17) and the clinical picture of recurrent ACS despite DAPT. Clopidogrel is a prodrug requiring activation by CYP2C19. Reduced activation leads to treatment failure (thrombotic events), while increased activation can lead to bleeding. The patient has one poor metabolizer allele and one ultrarapid metabolizer allele.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
*   **Clopidogrel Metabolism:** Clopidogrel is a thienopyridine prodrug that requires two-step hepatic oxidation by cytochrome P450 enzymes, primarily CYP2C19, to form its active thiol metabolite. This active metabolite irreversibly binds to the P2Y12 receptor on platelets, inhibiting ADP-mediated platelet aggregation.
*   **CYP2C19 Polymorphisms:** Genetic variations (polymorphisms) in the *CYP2C19* gene significantly affect enzyme activity.
    *   *CYP2C19*1 is the wild-type allele (extensive metabolizer).
    *   *CYP2C19*2 is a loss-of-function allele (poor metabolizer) due to a frameshift mutation (636G>A, resulting in Glu212Stop). Individuals homozygous for *CYP2C19*2 or heterozygous for *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*1 have significantly reduced CYP2C19 activity, leading to decreased active metabolite formation and reduced antiplatelet effect. This increases the risk of stent thrombosis and other thrombotic events.
    *   *CYP2C19*17 is a gain-of-function allele (ultrarapid metabolizer) due to a duplication of the *CYP2C19* gene (CYP2C19*17dup). Individuals homozygous for *CYP2C19*17 or heterozygous for *CYP2C19*17/*CYP2C19*1 have significantly increased CYP2C19 activity, leading to increased active metabolite formation. This can result in enhanced platelet inhibition and an increased risk of bleeding.
*   **Haplotype Concept:** A haplotype is a set of DNA variations (polymorphisms) that tend to be inherited together. In this case, the patient has a specific genotype (*CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17). This combination represents a compound heterozygote state. The *CYP2C19*2 allele leads to reduced clopidogrel activation, while the *CYP2C19*17 allele leads to increased clopidogrel activation. The net effect in a heterozygote is often intermediate metabolism, but the variability can be significant. However, the *CYP2C19*2 allele significantly impairs activation. The presence of even one *CYP2C19*2 allele is strongly associated with reduced clopidogrel efficacy and increased risk of thrombotic events, especially in patients with ACS and stent placement. The *CYP2C19*17 allele increases activation, but the *CYP2C19*2 allele's effect on reducing activation is clinically dominant in terms of treatment failure risk. Therefore, the patient likely has suboptimal clopidogrel activation due to the *CYP2C19*2 allele, leading to recurrent ACS.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. The *CYP2C19*2 allele leads to *decreased* production of the active metabolite, not increased. Increased active metabolite would be associated with the *CYP2C19*17 allele, potentially causing bleeding, not recurrent thrombosis due to inadequate inhibition.
- (C): Incorrect. While *CYP2C19* polymorphisms can influence statin metabolism (e.g., *CYP2C9* affects simvastatin, *CYP3A4* affects atorvastatin), the *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype is primarily linked to clopidogrel metabolism and platelet function, not directly to statin sensitivity causing myopathy misinterpreted as ACS. The vignette describes ACS symptoms (chest pain, troponin elevation, EKG changes), not myopathy.
- (D): Incorrect. While stent thrombosis is a risk associated with inadequate clopidogrel activation (due to *CYP2C19* loss-of-function alleles like *CYP2C19*2), the *CYP2C19*2/*CYP2C19*17 genotype itself is not primarily associated with impaired endothelial function causing stent thrombosis. The mechanism is reduced platelet inhibition due to insufficient active metabolite formation from clopidogrel. The *CYP2C19*2 allele is the key factor here.
- (E): Incorrect. Enhanced platelet aggregation would be expected with *reduced* clopidogrel activation, not enhanced. The *CYP2C19*17 allele leads to *increased* active metabolite and thus *decreased* platelet aggregation (increased inhibition), potentially increasing bleeding risk. The patient's recurrent ACS indicates inadequate platelet inhibition, not enhanced aggregation.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The *CYP2C19* gene exhibits significant genetic polymorphism affecting clopidogrel metabolism. Loss-of-function alleles (e.g., *CYP2C19*2) lead to reduced active metabolite formation, decreased platelet inhibition, and increased risk of thrombotic events (like stent thrombosis or recurrent ACS). Gain-of-function alleles (e.g., *CYP2C19*17) lead to increased active metabolite formation, enhanced platelet inhibition, and increased risk of bleeding. Patients with ACS treated with clopidogrel should ideally be genotyped for *CYP2C19* to guide therapy (e.g., alternative antiplatelet agents like prasugrel or ticagrelor, which are not CYP2C19 substrates, may be considered for *CYP2C19*2 carriers). Key differentials include other causes of stent thrombosis (e.g., non-adherence, other medications), but the genetic information points directly to impaired clopidogrel activation.

<a id='question-079'></a>

### Question 079: Haplotype Phasing and Recombination Events
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding haplotype phasing, recombination events, and their application in mapping disease genes, specifically identifying crossover breakpoints between closely linked markers. (理解 haplotype 分相、重组事件及其在疾病基因定位中的应用，特别是识别紧密连锁标记之间的交叉互换断点。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old woman presents to a genetics clinic due to a family history of early-onset Alzheimer's disease (AD). Her mother developed symptoms at age 45, and her maternal grandmother also had early-onset AD. The patient herself is asymptomatic but wishes to understand her genetic risk. Family history reveals no affected males. Genetic testing was performed on the patient and her mother. The patient is heterozygous for two closely linked polymorphic markers, D1-100 and D1-110, located on chromosome 1. The patient's genotype is D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> on one chromosome and D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> on the other chromosome. Her mother, who has early-onset AD, also tested positive for the AD mutation (APP gene) and is heterozygous for the same two markers, with the genotype D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>. The recombination frequency between D1-100 and D1-110 is known to be 5%. The patient's father is unaffected and has the genotype D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup>. Based on the patient's haplotype and her mother's genotype, which of the following statements regarding the recombination event that occurred during gamete formation in the patient's mother is most likely correct?

(A) Recombination occurred between D1-100 and D1-110 on the chromosome carrying the AD mutation allele, resulting in a gamete with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype.
(B) Recombination occurred between D1-100 and D1-110 on the chromosome carrying the AD mutation allele, resulting in a gamete with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype.
(C) Recombination did not occur between D1-100 and D1-110 on the chromosome carrying the AD mutation allele; the patient inherited the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype directly from her mother.
(D) Recombination occurred between D1-100 and D1-110 on the chromosome *not* carrying the AD mutation allele, resulting in a gamete with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype.
(E) Recombination occurred between D1-100 and D1-110 on the chromosome *not* carrying the AD mutation allele, resulting in a gamete with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype.

#### Correct Answer: (C)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient's Genotype:** D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> and D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>. This indicates she is heterozygous for both markers and has two different haplotypes.
- **Mother's Genotype:** D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>. She has the AD mutation and is homozygous for the D1-100<sup>A</sup> allele and the D1-110<sup>A</sup> allele. This means she carries the haplotype D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> on both chromosomes.
- **Father's Genotype:** D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup>. He is homozygous for the D1-100<sup>B</sup> allele and the D1-110<sup>B</sup> allele. This means he carries the haplotype D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> on both chromosomes.
- **Patient's Inheritance:** The patient must have inherited one chromosome (haplotype) from her mother and one chromosome (haplotype) from her father.
- **Mother's Contribution:** The mother can only contribute chromosomes with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype.
- **Father's Contribution:** The father can only contribute chromosomes with the D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype.
- **Patient's Haplotypes:** The patient has the haplotypes D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> (inherited from her mother) and D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> (inherited from her father).
- **The Question:** Asks about the recombination event that occurred in the mother's gamete formation, leading to the patient inheriting the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype.
- **Key Deduction:** Since the mother is homozygous (D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>), she can only produce gametes with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype. The patient inherited one chromosome from her mother, which must be D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>. Therefore, no recombination occurred on the chromosome carrying the AD mutation allele that was passed to the patient.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **Haplotype:** A set of DNA variations (polymorphisms) that tend to be inherited together. In this case, the markers D1-100 and D1-110 are polymorphic, meaning they have different alleles (A and B).
- **Phasing:** Determining which alleles are located on the same chromosome (i.e., determining the haplotype). The patient's genotype (D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> and D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>) indicates she has two distinct haplotypes.
- **Recombination:** The exchange of genetic material between homologous chromosomes during meiosis (prophase I). This process shuffles alleles, creating new combinations of haplotypes.
- **Crossover:** The physical exchange of chromosome segments during recombination.
- **Marker Linkage:** Markers located close together on a chromosome are less likely to be separated by recombination. The recombination frequency between D1-100 and D1-110 is 5%, indicating they are relatively closely linked.
- **Patient's Inheritance:** The patient received one chromosome 1 from her mother and one from her father.
    - From her father (D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup>), she received the D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype.
    - From her mother (D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>), she received the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype.
- **Mother's Gamete:** The mother has the genotype D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>. She can only produce gametes carrying the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype.
- **Recombination Analysis:** For the patient to inherit the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype from her mother, the chromosome carrying the AD mutation (and the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype) must have been transmitted without recombination. If recombination had occurred between D1-100 and D1-110 on this chromosome, the mother would have produced a gamete with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype (or vice versa), which is not possible given her genotype. Therefore, no recombination occurred on the chromosome carrying the AD mutation allele that was passed to the patient.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. Recombination between D1-100 and D1-110 on the chromosome carrying the AD mutation allele would result in a gamete with the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype (or D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>), which the mother cannot produce because she is homozygous D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>.
- (B): Incorrect. Similar to (A), recombination on the mother's chromosome would produce a gamete with a haplotype different from D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup>, which is impossible for the mother.
- (D): Incorrect. The patient inherited the D1-100<sup>B</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype from her father, not her mother. The question asks about the recombination event in the mother's gamete formation.
- (E): Incorrect. Similar to (D), the patient inherited the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>B</sup> haplotype from her father, not her mother. The question asks about the recombination event in the mother's gamete formation. Furthermore, recombination on the father's chromosome is irrelevant to the patient inheriting the D1-100<sup>A</sup>/D1-110<sup>A</sup> haplotype from her mother.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **Takeaway:** Understand how to trace the inheritance of haplotypes from parents to offspring and how recombination events can separate linked markers. Recognize that a homozygous individual can only transmit haplotypes they possess.
- **Differentials:** This question tests linkage analysis and haplotype phasing. Related concepts include pedigree analysis, calculating recombination frequencies, and understanding the principles of genetic mapping. Distinguish this from questions about specific metabolic pathways or enzyme deficiencies. This question focuses purely on the mechanics of meiosis and inheritance of linked markers.
